People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I... I came in because I keep having these thoughts that maybe it would be better if I just didn’t wake up. Like, not in a dramatic way, just this heavy feeling when I’m lying there, like everyone would get some relief if I wasn’t here making things worse.

And sometimes it gets stronger than that. I start thinking that dying would be... I don’t know, like a fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family, and to my crew at work. I keep going over it in my head, all the ways I’ve let people down, and it feels like I shouldn’t get to just keep going like nothing happened. It’s been scaring me, but part of me also feels like I deserve it, and that’s why I’m here.

Like I’ll be lying there and that thought just sits there, and it feels almost calm sometimes, which scares me more.

And then other times it turns into this thing where I feel like dying would be the fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family and my crew. I keep going over it and over it, like I’ve caused damage and I shouldn’t get to just keep walking around while everyone else has to deal with me. So… I can’t give you a clean number of days or weeks, but it’s been on my mind enough, and heavy enough, that I knew I probably shouldn’t keep it to myself anymore.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah... that’s changed a lot. I used to have things that were mine, you know? Like I used to paint—not even anything fancy, just colors and shapes and sometimes little landscapes—and I could lose a whole evening in it. Now the paints just sit there, and when I look at them it feels like... like supplies that belong to some other woman.

I used to cook big meals too, especially on Sundays or if people were coming over. I’d make too much food and complain about the mess, but I liked it. Now even thinking about chopping things or planning a meal feels like this huge job, like a chore I’ve already failed before I start.

And I used to run before jobs sometimes, early, just to get my body going before flooring work. I wasn’t some marathon person, but it made me feel steady. I don’t really care about that now. Same with theater rehearsals—I used to show up, even if I was tired from work, because it felt good to be part of something. Lately I miss them and I don’t even feel much about it. It’s like all those things are from somebody else’s life, and I’m just... not interested enough to reach for them.

Yes. Definitely more than two weeks. It’s been going on for… about a year and two months now, I think. It started a few months after my husband’s injury, and at first I kept telling myself I was just tired, or stressed, or that things were hard and anybody would feel worn down.

even though I'm not trying to lose weight.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: By the time I description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I'm the reason my husband got worse, the crew lost work, and clients' homes got messed up, like I poisoned everything I touched and deserve whatever happens to me.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I came in because I keep thinking it would be better if I didn't wake up, and sometimes I feel like dying would be the fair punishment for what I've done to my family and my crew.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During the months when I was barely eating, wishing I might not wake up, and blaming myself for everything, I was also hearing that man's voice thoughts about death have been there, even if some weeks were less crushing than others.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of several weeks when I was getting up, eating a little better, and not crying all day, but I still heard the voice at work and stayed convinced clients and suppliers were tracking me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I had to stop leading flooring installations because I couldn't safely manage the crew, clients, or job decisions, and my apprentice and husband started stepping in before I ruined work or put someone at risk.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about a year and five months, since my husband's back injury made him mostly housebound.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The voices and fixed ideas about being tracked through jobs and messages have been happening for around eleven months.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The depression has been with me for about a year and two months, since a few months after my husband's injury.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 40 / carpet_floor_or_tile_installer_or_finisher / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S032\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up or feeling like you deserve punishment?\n\nPatient: It’s been... I used to have things that were mine, you know? Like I used to paint—not even anything fancy, just colors and shapes and sometimes little landscapes—and I could lose a whole evening in it. I’d make too much food and complain about the mess, but I liked it. It’s like all those things are from somebody else’s life, and I’m just... not interested enough to reach for them.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or had a loss of interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Yes. Definitely more than two weeks. It’s been going on for… about a year and two months now, I think. It started a few months after my husband’s injury, and at first I kept telling myself I was just tired, or stressed, or that things were hard and anybody would feel worn down.\n\nBut it didn’t lift. It just settled in and stayed. The depression, and that not caring about things I used to care about… it’s been there most of that time. Some days are a little less bad than others, but it hasn’t felt like I came back to myself for any real stretch. I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had hard times before, definitely. I’ve had stretches where I was sad or worn down or didn’t feel like myself, but I don’t know if I’d call them “periods of depression like this,” not the way this has been.

This feels... heavier, and more like it got into everything. Before, if I was upset, I could usually point to why, or I could still kind of push through and have parts of my life that felt normal. With this, it’s harder for me to separate out. So I guess my honest answer is I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had anything quite like this before, but I don’t want to say that too confidently because sometimes when you look back, you realize you were doing worse than you admitted at the time.

description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I came in because I keep thinking it would be better if I didn't wake up, and sometimes I cleanly. I mean, I’ve had hard times before, definitely. I’ve had stretches where I was sad or worn down or didn’t feel like myself, but I don’t know if I’d call them “periods of depression like this,” not the way this has been.\n\nThis feels... heavier, and more like it got into everything. Before, if I was upset, I could usually point to why, or I could still kind of push through and have parts of my life that felt normal. With this, it’s harder for me to separate out. So I guess my honest answer is I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had anything quite like this before, but I don’t want to say that too confidently because sometimes when you look back, you realize you were doing worse than you admitted at the time.\n\nDoctor: Was there something specific, like your husband's injury, that you think might have triggered you starting to feel this way over a year ago?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I can’t say it was like a switch flipped the exact day or anything, but when I look back, it really does seem tied to what happened with my husband’s injury. For a little while after it happened, I was just... functioning, I guess. Handling things, trying to be practical, telling myself we just had to get through the next thing and the next thing.\n\nBut then a few months after that, it was like everything caught up with me. That’s when the depression really settled in, and it just didn’t leave. So it’s been about a year and two months now, since a few months after his injury, that I’ve been feeling this way. It’s hard to explain because at first I thought it was just stress, but it became something that stayed with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to normal between episodes of depression?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s part of what worries me, I guess. It hasn’t felt like there were separate episodes with a clean break in between, where I was back to my normal self for a while and then it started again.\n\nSince a few months after my husband’s injury, it’s just been kind of… there. About a year and two months now. Some days are maybe a little lighter, or I can get through things better, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt completely normal in between. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t really know how to count it, honestly. If you mean have I had other times in my life where I was sad or down for a while, then yes, I guess there have been times like that. But if you mean a real period of depression like this one, where it felt like it took over my whole life and didn’t really let up, then I’m not sure I can say that.

I keep trying to look back and separate things into neat chunks, like “that was one depression” and “this is another,” but my memory of those times isn’t that clear in that way. I know I’ve had rough patches before, and I know I didn’t always handle things well, but this current one feels different enough that I don’t want to say I’ve had multiple periods like this if I’m not sure.

So… I guess my answer is I’m not sure. Maybe I’ve had low periods before, but I don’t know that I’ve had more than one true period of depression in the way you mean.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I... I came in because I keep having these thoughts that maybe it would be better if I just didn’t wake up. Like, not in a dramatic way, just this heavy feeling when I’m lying there, like everyone would get some relief if I wasn’t here making things worse.\n\nAnd sometimes it gets stronger than that. I start thinking that dying would be... I don’t know, like a fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family, and to my crew at work. I keep going over it in my head, all the ways I’ve let people down, and it feels like I shouldn’t get to just keep going like nothing happened. It’s been scaring me, but part of me also feels like I deserve it, and that’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up or feeling like you deserve punishment?\n\nPatient: It’s been... I don’t know exactly how to put a start date on it, because it kind of crept in. But lately it’s been enough that I came in because I keep thinking it would be better if I didn’t wake up. Like I’ll be lying there and that thought just sits there, and it feels almost calm sometimes, which scares me more.\n\nAnd then other times it turns into this thing where I feel like dying would be the fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family and my crew. I keep going over it and over it, like I’ve caused damage and I shouldn’t get to just keep walking around while everyone else has to deal with me. So… I can’t give you a clean number of days or weeks, but it’s been on my mind enough, and heavy enough, that I knew I probably shouldn’t keep it to myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah... that’s changed a lot. I used to have things that were mine, you know? Like I used to paint—not even anything fancy, just colors and shapes and sometimes little landscapes—and I could lose a whole evening in it. Now the paints just sit there, and when I look at them it feels like... like supplies that belong to some other woman.\n\nI used to cook big meals too, especially on Sundays or if people were coming over. I’d make too much food and complain about the mess, but I liked it. I keep going over it in my head, all the ways I’ve let people down, and it feels like I shouldn’t get to just keep going like nothing happened. It’s been scaring me, but part of me also feels like I deserve it, and that’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up or feeling like you deserve punishment?\n\nPatient: It’s been... I don’t know exactly how to put a start date on it, because it kind of crept in. But lately it’s been enough that I came in because I keep thinking it would be better if I didn’t wake up. Like I’ll be lying there and that thought just sits there, and it feels almost calm sometimes, which scares me more.\n\nAnd then other times it turns into this thing where I feel like dying would be the fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family and my crew. I keep going over it and over it, like I’ve caused damage and I shouldn’t get to just keep walking around while everyone else has to deal with me. So… I can’t give you a clean number of days or weeks, but it’s been on my mind enough, and heavy enough, that I knew I probably shouldn’t keep it to myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah... that’s changed a lot. I used to have things that were mine, you know? Like I used to paint—not even anything fancy, just colors and shapes and sometimes little landscapes—and I could lose a whole evening in it. Now the paints just sit there, and when I look at them it feels like... like supplies that belong to some other woman.\n\nI used to cook big meals too, especially on Sundays or if people were coming over. I’d make too much food and complain about the mess, but I liked it. Now even thinking about chopping things or planning a meal feels like this huge job, like a chore I’ve already failed before I start.\n\nAnd I used to run before jobs sometimes, early, just to get my body going before flooring work. I wasn’t some marathon person, but it made me feel steady. I don’t really care about that now. Same with theater rehearsals—I used to show up, even if I was tired from work, because it felt good to be part of something. Lately I miss them and I don’t even feel much about it. It’s like all those things are from somebody else’s life, and I’m just... not interested enough to reach for them.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or had a loss of interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Yes. Definitely more than two weeks. It’s been going on for… about a year and two months now, I think. It just settled in and stayed. The depression, and that not caring about things I used to care about… it’s been there most of that time. Some days are a little less bad than others, but it hasn’t felt like I came back to myself for any real stretch. It’s been over a year of feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of depression like this before?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had hard times before, definitely. I’ve had stretches where I was sad or worn down or didn’t feel like myself, but I don’t know if I’d call them “periods of depression like this,” not the way this has been.\n\nThis feels... heavier, and more like it got into everything. Before, if I was upset, I could usually point to why, or I could still kind of push through and have parts of my life that felt normal. With this, it’s harder for me to separate out. So I guess my honest answer is I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had anything quite like this before, but I don’t want to say that too confidently because sometimes when you look back, you realize you were doing worse than you admitted at the time.\n\nDoctor: Was there something specific, like your husband's injury, that you think might have triggered you starting to feel this way over a year ago?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I can’t say it was like a switch flipped the exact day or anything, but when I look back, it really does seem tied to what happened with my husband’s injury. For a little while after it happened, I was just... functioning, I guess. Handling things, trying to be practical, telling myself we just had to get through the next thing and the next thing.\n\nBut then a few months after that, it was like everything caught up with me. That’s when the depression really settled in, and it just didn’t leave. So it’s been about a year and two months now, since a few months after his injury, that I’ve been feeling this way. It’s hard to explain because at first I thought it was just stress, but it became something that stayed with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to normal between episodes of depression?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s part of what worries me, I guess. It hasn’t felt like there were separate episodes with a clean break in between, where I was back to my normal self for a while and then it started again.\n\nSince a few months after my husband’s injury, it’s just been kind of… there. About a year and two months now. Some days are maybe a little lighter, or I can get through things better, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt completely normal in between. It’s more like it settled over me and stayed, instead of coming and going in clear episodes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period of depression in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to count it, honestly. If you mean have I had other times in my life where I was sad or down for a while, then yes, I guess there have been times like that. But if you mean a real period of depression like this one, where it felt like it took over my whole life and didn’t really let up, then I’m not sure I can say that.\n\nI keep trying to look back and separate things into neat chunks, like “that was one depression” and “this is another,” but my memory of those times isn’t that clear in that way. I know I’ve had rough patches before, and I know I didn’t always handle things well, but this current one feels different enough that I don’t want to say I’ve had multiple periods like this if I’m not sure.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I’m not sure. Maybe I’ve had low periods before, but I don’t know that I’ve had more than one true period of depression in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current feelings of depression are a return of the same depression you felt after your husband's injury, or do you feel like it's a new episode?\n\nPatient: I think it’s the same depression, honestly. It doesn’t feel like something that went away and then came back new. It’s more like it started a few months after my husband’s injury and it’s just… stayed with me since then.\n\nI keep trying to think of it in terms of episodes, because I know that’s what you’re asking, but I don’t really feel like there was a clear ending point. It’s been about a year and two months now, and even when it shifts a little from day to day, it still feels like the same thing I’ve been carrying since then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Before that? I’m not really sure. I’ve had times in my life where I was down, or stressed, or just not myself for a while, but I don’t know if I would call them depression in the same way this has been.

I keep thinking maybe I’m supposed to be able to look back and say, “Yes, that was an episode,” but it doesn’t feel that clear to me. Some of it just feels like normal hard periods in life, or times where I was overwhelmed, and I got through them somehow. This one feels more like it has a clear starting point after what happened with my husband, and then it just kept going.

So I guess my honest answer is… I don’t know. I don’t have a definite period before that where I can say, yes, that was depression. There may have been low times, but nothing I can clearly identify the way I can with this one.

It started a few months after my husband’s injury, and at first I kept telling myself I was just tired, or stressed, or that things were hard and anybody would feel worn down.\n\nBut it didn’t lift. It just settled in and stayed. The depression, and that not caring about things I used to care about… it’s been there most of that time. Some days are a little less bad than others, but it hasn’t felt like I came back to myself for any real stretch. It’s been over a year of feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of depression like this before?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had hard times before, definitely. I’ve had stretches where I was sad or worn down or didn’t feel like myself, but I don’t know if I’d call them “periods of depression like this,” not the way this has been.\n\nThis feels... heavier, and more like it got into everything. Before, if I was upset, I could usually point to why, or I could still kind of push through and have parts of my life that felt normal. With this, it’s harder for me to separate out. So I guess my honest answer is I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had anything quite like this before, but I don’t want to say that too confidently because sometimes when you look back, you realize you were doing worse than you admitted at the time.\n\nDoctor: Was there something specific, like your husband's injury, that you think might have triggered you starting to feel this way over a year ago?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I can’t say it was like a switch flipped the exact day or anything, but when I look back, it really does seem tied to what happened with my husband’s injury. For a little while after it happened, I was just... functioning, I guess. Handling things, trying to be practical, telling myself we just had to get through the next thing and the next thing.\n\nBut then a few months after that, it was like everything caught up with me. That’s when the depression really settled in, and it just didn’t leave. So it’s been about a year and two months now, since a few months after his injury, that I’ve been feeling this way. It’s hard to explain because at first I thought it was just stress, but it became something that stayed with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to normal between episodes of depression?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s part of what worries me, I guess. It hasn’t felt like there were separate episodes with a clean break in between, where I was back to my normal self for a while and then it started again.\n\nSince a few months after my husband’s injury, it’s just been kind of… there. About a year and two months now. Some days are maybe a little lighter, or I can get through things better, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt completely normal in between. It’s more like it settled over me and stayed, instead of coming and going in clear episodes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period of depression in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to count it, honestly. If you mean have I had other times in my life where I was sad or down for a while, then yes, I guess there have been times like that. But if you mean a real period of depression like this one, where it felt like it took over my whole life and didn’t really let up, then I’m not sure I can say that.\n\nI keep trying to look back and separate things into neat chunks, like “that was one depression” and “this is another,” but my memory of those times isn’t that clear in that way. I know I’ve had rough patches before, and I know I didn’t always handle things well, but this current one feels different enough that I don’t want to say I’ve had multiple periods like this if I’m not sure.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I’m not sure. Maybe I’ve had low periods before, but I don’t know that I’ve had more than one true period of depression in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current feelings of depression are a return of the same depression you felt after your husband's injury, or do you feel like it's a new episode?\n\nPatient: I think it’s the same depression, honestly. It doesn’t feel like something that went away and then came back new. It’s more like it started a few months after my husband’s injury and it’s just… stayed with me since then.\n\nI keep trying to think of it in terms of episodes, because I know that’s what you’re asking, but I don’t really feel like there was a clear ending point. It’s been about a year and two months now, and even when it shifts a little from day to day, it still feels like the same thing I’ve been carrying since then.\n\nDoctor: Are there any current stressors or difficult situations in your life that you feel are contributing to how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that, because I don’t know if I can point to one current thing and say, “That’s what’s causing it.” I mean, life is stressful in the normal ways, I guess, and there are always things to worry about, but I don’t know that there’s some new situation happening right now that explains why I feel the way I do.\n\nIt’s more like… the feeling is already there, and then ordinary things feel heavier than they should. So I can’t tell if the stress is contributing to it, or if I’m just less able to handle stress because of how I’ve been feeling. I don’t want to make something up just to have an answer.\n\nSo, no, not anything clear that I’ve noticed. Nothing specific comes to mind that feels like, “This is the current stressor making everything worse.”\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of depression before the one that started after your husband's injury?\n\nPatient: Before that? I’m not really sure. I’ve had times in my life where I was down, or stressed, or just not myself for a while, but I don’t know if I would call them depression in the same way this has been.\n\nI keep thinking maybe I’m supposed to be able to look back and say, “Yes, that was an episode,” but it doesn’t feel that clear to me. Some of it just feels like normal hard periods in life, or times where I was overwhelmed, and I got through them somehow. This one feels more like it has a clear starting point after what happened with my husband, and then it just kept going.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is… I don’t know. I don’t have a definite period before that where I can say, yes, that was depression. There may have been low times, but nothing I can clearly identify the way I can with this one.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

I’ve had times in my life where I was down, or stressed, or just not myself for a while, but I don’t know if I would call them depression in the same way this has been.\n\nI keep thinking maybe I’m supposed to be able to look back and say, “Yes, that was an episode,” but it doesn’t feel that clear to me. Some of it just feels like normal hard periods in life, or times where I was overwhelmed, and I got through them somehow. This one feels more like it has a clear starting point after what happened with my husband, and then it just kept going.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is… I don’t know. I don’t have a definite period before that where I can say, yes, that was depression. There may have been low times, but nothing I can clearly identify the way I can with this one.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 40 / carpet_floor_or_tile_installer_or_finisher / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Weight_Appetite_Change\nmanifestation: \"Most days I forget to eat until late or I take a few bites and feel sick, and my work pants have gotten loose even though I'm not trying to lose weight.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself all the time, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially have gotten loose, and I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything.

It’s more that most days I just don’t really eat right. I’ll forget to eat until really late, and then when I do finally try, I’ll take a few bites and feel kind of sick, like my stomach just doesn’t want it. So I know I must be losing some weight, or at least something has changed, because my pants don’t fit the way they used to.

I don’t know exactly how to put a start date on it, because it kind of crept in. But lately it’s been enough that I came in because I keep thinking it would be better if I didn’t wake up. I keep going over it and over it, like I’ve caused damage and I shouldn’t get to just keep walking around while everyone else has to deal with me. So… I can’t give you a clean number of days or weeks, but it’s been on my mind enough, and heavy enough, that I knew I probably shouldn’t keep it to myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah... that’s changed a lot. I used to have things that were mine, you know? Like I used to paint—not even anything fancy, just colors and shapes and sometimes little landscapes—and I could lose a whole evening in it. Now the paints just sit there, and when I look at them it feels like... like supplies that belong to some other woman.\n\nI used to cook big meals too, especially on Sundays or if people were coming over. Now even thinking about chopping things or planning a meal feels like this huge job, like a chore I’ve already failed before I start.\n\nAnd I used to run before jobs sometimes, early, just to get my body going before flooring work. I wasn’t some marathon person, but it made me feel steady. I don’t really care about that now. Same with theater rehearsals—I used to show up, even if I was tired from work, because it felt good to be part of something. Lately I miss them and I don’t even feel much about it. It’s like all those things are from somebody else’s life, and I’m just... not interested enough to reach for them.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or had a loss of interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Yes. Definitely more than two weeks. It’s been going on for… about a year and two months now, I think. It started a few months after my husband’s injury, and at first I kept telling myself I was just tired, or stressed, or that things were hard and anybody would feel worn down.\n\nBut it didn’t lift. It just settled in and stayed. The depression, and that not caring about things I used to care about… it’s been there most of that time. It’s hard to explain because at first I thought it was just stress, but it became something that stayed with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to normal between episodes of depression?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It hasn’t felt like there were separate episodes with a clean break in between, where I was back to my normal self for a while and then it started again.\n\nSince a few months after my husband’s injury, it’s just been kind of… there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I… I’ve thought about dying, yes. That’s part of why I came in, because it’s been scaring me how often it comes up in my head. A lot of mornings, or even at night before I sleep, I’ll think it would just be better if I didn’t wake up. Like if I could just not have to keep being here and keep being this person who has caused so much hurt.

And sometimes it gets tied up with this feeling that dying would be… I don’t know, like the fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family and to my crew. Like I don’t deserve to just keep going like nothing happened. I keep replaying it and thinking maybe if I was gone, that would balance it somehow, or at least people wouldn’t have to deal with me anymore.

I don’t know if that answers exactly what you’re asking. It’s not like I have some clear speech prepared about it. It’s more that the thought comes in really hard, like, “You should be gone,” or “It would be better if you didn’t wake up.” And when I’m already feeling guilty, it starts to feel almost… deserved.

Before, if I was upset, I could usually point to why, or I could still kind of push through and have parts of my life that felt normal. With this, it’s harder for me to separate out. It’s hard to explain because at first I thought it was just stress, but it became something that stayed with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to normal between episodes of depression?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s part of what worries me, I guess. It hasn’t felt like there were separate episodes with a clean break in between, where I was back to my normal self for a while and then it started again.\n\nSince a few months after my husband’s injury, it’s just been kind of… there. About a year and two months now. Some days are maybe a little lighter, or I can get through things better, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt completely normal in between. It’s been over a year of feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of depression like this before?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had hard times before, definitely. I’ve had stretches where I was sad or worn down or didn’t feel like myself, but I don’t know if I’d call them “periods of depression like this,” not the way this has been.\n\nThis feels... heavier, and more like it got into everything. Before, if I was upset, I could usually point to why, or I could still kind of push through and have parts of my life that felt normal. With this, it’s harder for me to separate out. So I guess my honest answer is I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had anything quite like this before, but I don’t want to say that too confidently because sometimes when you look back, you realize you were doing worse than you admitted at the time.\n\nDoctor: Was there something specific, like your husband's injury, that you think might have triggered you starting to feel this way over a year ago?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I can’t say it was like a switch flipped the exact day or anything, but when I look back, it really does seem tied to what happened with my husband’s injury. For a little while after it happened, I was just... functioning, I guess. Handling things, trying to be practical, telling myself we just had to get through the next thing and the next thing.\n\nBut then a few months after that, it was like everything caught up with me. That’s when the depression really settled in, and it just didn’t leave. So it’s been about a year and two months now, since a few months after his injury, that I’ve been feeling this way. It’s hard to explain because at first I thought it was just stress, but it became something that stayed with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to normal between episodes of depression?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s part of what worries me, I guess. It hasn’t felt like there were separate episodes with a clean break in between, where I was back to my normal self for a while and then it started again.\n\nSince a few months after my husband’s injury, it’s just been kind of… there. About a year and two months now. Some days are maybe a little lighter, or I can get through things better, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt completely normal in between. It’s more like it settled over me and stayed, instead of coming and going in clear episodes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period of depression in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to count it, honestly. If you mean have I had other times in my life where I was sad or down for a while, then yes, I guess there have been times like that. But if you mean a real period of depression like this one, where it felt like it took over my whole life and didn’t really let up, then I’m not sure I can say that.\n\nI keep trying to look back and separate things into neat chunks, like “that was one depression” and “this is another,” but my memory of those times isn’t that clear in that way. I know I’ve had rough patches before, and I know I didn’t always handle things well, but this current one feels different enough that I don’t want to say I’ve had multiple periods like this if I’m not sure.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I’m not sure. Maybe I’ve had low periods before, but I don’t know that I’ve had more than one true period of depression in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current feelings of depression are a return of the same depression you felt after your husband's injury, or do you feel like it's a new episode?\n\nPatient: I think it’s the same depression, honestly. It doesn’t feel like something that went away and then came back new. It’s more like it started a few months after my husband’s injury and it’s just… stayed with me since then.\n\nI keep trying to think of it in terms of episodes, because I know that’s what you’re asking, but I don’t really feel like there was a clear ending point. It’s been about a year and two months now, and even when it shifts a little from day to day, it still feels like the same thing I’ve been carrying since then.\n\nDoctor: Are there any current stressors or difficult situations in your life that you feel are contributing to how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that, because I don’t know if I can point to one current thing and say, “That’s what’s causing it.” I mean, life is stressful in the normal ways, I guess, and there are always things to worry about, but I don’t know that there’s some new situation happening right now that explains why I feel the way I do.\n\nIt’s more like… the feeling is already there, and then ordinary things feel heavier than they should. So I can’t tell if the stress is contributing to it, or if I’m just less able to handle stress because of how I’ve been feeling. I don’t want to make something up just to have an answer.\n\nSo, no, not anything clear that I’ve noticed. Nothing specific comes to mind that feels like, “This is the current stressor making everything worse.”\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of depression before the one that started after your husband's injury?\n\nPatient: Before that? I’m not really sure. I’ve had times in my life where I was down, or stressed, or just not myself for a while, but I don’t know if I would call them depression in the same way this has been.\n\nI keep thinking maybe I’m supposed to be able to look back and say, “Yes, that was an episode,” but it doesn’t feel that clear to me. Some of it just feels like normal hard periods in life, or times where I was overwhelmed, and I got through them somehow. This one feels more like it has a clear starting point after what happened with my husband, and then it just kept going.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is… I don’t know. I don’t have a definite period before that where I can say, yes, that was depression. There may have been low times, but nothing I can clearly identify the way I can with this one.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself all the time, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially have gotten loose, and I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything.\n\nIt’s more that most days I just don’t really eat right. I’ll forget to eat until really late, and then when I do finally try, I’ll take a few bites and feel kind of sick, like my stomach just doesn’t want it. So I know I must be losing some weight, or at least something has changed, because my pants don’t fit the way they used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about how you would carry out the act of dying as a punishment for what you've done?\n\nPatient: I… I’ve thought about dying, yes. That’s part of why I came in, because it’s been scaring me how often it comes up in my head. A lot of mornings, or even at night before I sleep, I’ll think it would just be better if I didn’t wake up. Like if I could just not have to keep being here and keep being this person who has caused so much hurt.\n\nAnd sometimes it gets tied up with this feeling that dying would be… I don’t know, like the fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family and to my crew. Like I don’t deserve to just keep going like nothing happened. I keep replaying it and thinking maybe if I was gone, that would balance it somehow, or at least people wouldn’t have to deal with me anymore.\n\nI don’t know if that answers exactly what you’re asking. It’s not like I have some clear speech prepared about it. It’s more that the thought comes in really hard, like, “You should be gone,” or “It would be better if you didn’t wake up.” And when I’m already feeling guilty, it starts to feel almost… deserved.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know I kind of answered that already, but… I really don’t know how to separate it out looking back.

I’ve had hard stretches before, sure. Times where I was worn down, or unhappy, or just felt like I was dragging myself through things. Like, not in a dramatic way, just this heavy feeling when I’m lying there, like everyone would get some relief if I wasn’t here making things worse.\n\nAnd sometimes it gets stronger than that. It’s been scaring me, but part of me also feels like I deserve it, and that’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up or feeling like you deserve punishment?\n\nPatient: It’s been... I don’t know exactly how to put a start date on it, because it kind of crept in. But lately it’s been enough that I came in because I keep thinking it would be better if I didn’t wake up. Like I’ll be lying there and that thought just sits there, and it feels almost calm sometimes, which scares me more.\n\nAnd then other times it turns into this thing where I feel like dying would be the fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family and my crew. I keep going over it and over it, like I’ve caused damage and I shouldn’t get to just keep walking around while everyone else has to deal with me. So… I can’t give you a clean number of days or weeks, but it’s been on my mind enough, and heavy enough, that I knew I probably shouldn’t keep it to myself anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah... that’s changed a lot. I used to have things that were mine, you know? Like I used to paint—not even anything fancy, just colors and shapes and sometimes little landscapes—and I could lose a whole evening in it. Now the paints just sit there, and when I look at them it feels like... like supplies that belong to some other woman.\n\nI used to cook big meals too, especially on Sundays or if people were coming over. I’d make too much food and complain about the mess, but I liked it. Now even thinking about chopping things or planning a meal feels like this huge job, like a chore I’ve already failed before I start.\n\nAnd I used to run before jobs sometimes, early, just to get my body going before flooring work. I wasn’t some marathon person, but it made me feel steady. I don’t really care about that now. Same with theater rehearsals—I used to show up, even if I was tired from work, because it felt good to be part of something. Lately I miss them and I don’t even feel much about it. It’s like all those things are from somebody else’s life, and I’m just... not interested enough to reach for them.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or had a loss of interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Yes. Definitely more than two weeks. It’s been going on for… about a year and two months now, I think. It started a few months after my husband’s injury, and at first I kept telling myself I was just tired, or stressed, or that things were hard and anybody would feel worn down.\n\nBut it didn’t lift. It just settled in and stayed. The depression, and that not caring about things I used to care about… it’s been there most of that time. Some days are a little less bad than others, but it hasn’t felt like I came back to myself for any real stretch. It’s been over a year of feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of depression like this before?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I’ve had hard times before, definitely. I’ve had stretches where I was sad or worn down or didn’t feel like myself, but I don’t know if I’d call them “periods of depression like this,” not the way this has been.\n\nThis feels... heavier, and more like it got into everything. Before, if I was upset, I could usually point to why, or I could still kind of push through and have parts of my life that felt normal. With this, it’s harder for me to separate out. So I guess my honest answer is I’m not sure. I don’t think I’ve had anything quite like this before, but I don’t want to say that too confidently because sometimes when you look back, you realize you were doing worse than you admitted at the time.\n\nDoctor: Was there something specific, like your husband's injury, that you think might have triggered you starting to feel this way over a year ago?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I can’t say it was like a switch flipped the exact day or anything, but when I look back, it really does seem tied to what happened with my husband’s injury. For a little while after it happened, I was just... functioning, I guess. Handling things, trying to be practical, telling myself we just had to get through the next thing and the next thing.\n\nBut then a few months after that, it was like everything caught up with me. That’s when the depression really settled in, and it just didn’t leave. So it’s been about a year and two months now, since a few months after his injury, that I’ve been feeling this way. It’s hard to explain because at first I thought it was just stress, but it became something that stayed with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to normal between episodes of depression?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s part of what worries me, I guess. It hasn’t felt like there were separate episodes with a clean break in between, where I was back to my normal self for a while and then it started again.\n\nSince a few months after my husband’s injury, it’s just been kind of… there. About a year and two months now. Some days are maybe a little lighter, or I can get through things better, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt completely normal in between. It’s more like it settled over me and stayed, instead of coming and going in clear episodes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period of depression in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to count it, honestly. If you mean have I had other times in my life where I was sad or down for a while, then yes, I guess there have been times like that. But if you mean a real period of depression like this one, where it felt like it took over my whole life and didn’t really let up, then I’m not sure I can say that.\n\nI keep trying to look back and separate things into neat chunks, like “that was one depression” and “this is another,” but my memory of those times isn’t that clear in that way. I know I’ve had rough patches before, and I know I didn’t always handle things well, but this current one feels different enough that I don’t want to say I’ve had multiple periods like this if I’m not sure.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I’m not sure. Maybe I’ve had low periods before, but I don’t know that I’ve had more than one true period of depression in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current feelings of depression are a return of the same depression you felt after your husband's injury, or do you feel like it's a new episode?\n\nPatient: I think it’s the same depression, honestly. It doesn’t feel like something that went away and then came back new. It’s more like it started a few months after my husband’s injury and it’s just… stayed with me since then.\n\nI keep trying to think of it in terms of episodes, because I know that’s what you’re asking, but I don’t really feel like there was a clear ending point. It’s been about a year and two months now, and even when it shifts a little from day to day, it still feels like the same thing I’ve been carrying since then.\n\nDoctor: Are there any current stressors or difficult situations in your life that you feel are contributing to how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that, because I don’t know if I can point to one current thing and say, “That’s what’s causing it.” I mean, life is stressful in the normal ways, I guess, and there are always things to worry about, but I don’t know that there’s some new situation happening right now that explains why I feel the way I do.\n\nIt’s more like… the feeling is already there, and then ordinary things feel heavier than they should. So I can’t tell if the stress is contributing to it, or if I’m just less able to handle stress because of how I’ve been feeling. I don’t want to make something up just to have an answer.\n\nSo, no, not anything clear that I’ve noticed. Nothing specific comes to mind that feels like, “This is the current stressor making everything worse.”\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of depression before the one that started after your husband's injury?\n\nPatient: Before that? I’m not really sure. I’ve had times in my life where I was down, or stressed, or just not myself for a while, but I don’t know if I would call them depression in the same way this has been.\n\nI keep thinking maybe I’m supposed to be able to look back and say, “Yes, that was an episode,” but it doesn’t feel that clear to me. Some of it just feels like normal hard periods in life, or times where I was overwhelmed, and I got through them somehow. This one feels more like it has a clear starting point after what happened with my husband, and then it just kept going.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is… I don’t know. I don’t have a definite period before that where I can say, yes, that was depression. There may have been low times, but nothing I can clearly identify the way I can with this one.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself all the time, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially have gotten loose, and I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything.\n\nIt’s more that most days I just don’t really eat right. I’ll forget to eat until really late, and then when I do finally try, I’ll take a few bites and feel kind of sick, like my stomach just doesn’t want it. So I know I must be losing some weight, or at least something has changed, because my pants don’t fit the way they used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about how you would carry out the act of dying as a punishment for what you've done?\n\nPatient: I… I’ve thought about dying, yes. That’s part of why I came in, because it’s been scaring me how often it comes up in my head. A lot of mornings, or even at night before I sleep, I’ll think it would just be better if I didn’t wake up. Like if I could just not have to keep being here and keep being this person who has caused so much hurt.\n\nAnd sometimes it gets tied up with this feeling that dying would be… I don’t know, like the fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family and to my crew. Like I don’t deserve to just keep going like nothing happened. I keep replaying it and thinking maybe if I was gone, that would balance it somehow, or at least people wouldn’t have to deal with me anymore.\n\nI don’t know if that answers exactly what you’re asking. It’s not like I have some clear speech prepared about it. It’s more that the thought comes in really hard, like, “You should be gone,” or “It would be better if you didn’t wake up.” And when I’m already feeling guilty, it starts to feel almost… deserved.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any periods of depression before your husband's injury?\n\nPatient: I know I kind of answered that already, but… I really don’t know how to separate it out looking back.\n\nI’ve had hard stretches before, sure. Times where I was worn down, or unhappy, or just felt like I was dragging myself through things. But I can’t point to one and say, “That was definitely depression,” not the way I can point to what happened after my husband got hurt. That felt like there was a before and after, if that makes sense.\n\nBefore then, I think I would’ve just called it stress, or being tired, or life being heavy for a while. Maybe I wasn’t paying enough attention to myself, I don’t know. But I don’t have a clear episode in my mind that I can say started here and ended there. So my honest answer is I’m not sure. There were low times, but nothing I can confidently name as depression before his injury.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… there was a period like that. Not like I was “fine,” exactly, but compared to how bad it had been, it was better enough that I noticed it.

For maybe a few weeks, I was getting up more regularly instead of staying in bed half the day. I was eating a little better too — not normal meals all the time, but at least more than just picking at something or forgetting completely. And I wasn’t crying all day every day during that stretch. I could get through more of the day without falling apart, and I remember thinking maybe I was starting to come back to myself a little.

But it wasn’t like everything cleared up. Even during that better stretch, I was still hearing the voice at work, and I still really believed clients and suppliers were tracking me. That part didn’t go away just because I was functioning a little better. I could show up and do more things, but underneath it I was still scared and still convinced something was happening around me.

So… yes, there was a better period after his injury, but it wasn’t back to normal. It was more like I could move around and eat and not cry constantly, while that other stuff was still there.

Like I’ll be lying there and that thought just sits there, and it feels almost calm sometimes, which scares me more.\n\nAnd then other times it turns into this thing where I feel like dying would be the fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family and my crew. I keep going over it and over it, like I’ve caused damage and I shouldn’t get to just keep walking around while everyone else has to deal with me. I used to have things that were mine, you know? Like I used to paint—not even anything fancy, just colors and shapes and sometimes little landscapes—and I could lose a whole evening in it. Now the paints just sit there, and when I look at them it feels like... like supplies that belong to some other woman.\n\nI used to cook big meals too, especially on Sundays or if people were coming over. I wasn’t some marathon person, but it made me feel steady. I don’t really care about that now. It’s like all those things are from somebody else’s life, and I’m just... not interested enough to reach for them.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or had a loss of interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Yes. Definitely more than two weeks. It’s been going on for… about a year and two months now, I think. It started a few months after my husband’s injury, and at first I kept telling myself I was just tired, or stressed, or that things were hard and anybody would feel worn down.\n\nBut it didn’t lift. I mean, I’ve had hard times before, definitely. I’ve had stretches where I was sad or worn down or didn’t feel like myself, but I don’t know if I’d call them “periods of depression like this,” not the way this has been.\n\nThis feels... heavier, and more like it got into everything. Before, if I was upset, I could usually point to why, or I could still kind of push through and have parts of my life that felt normal. With this, it’s harder for me to separate out. I don’t think I’ve had anything quite like this before, but I don’t want to say that too confidently because sometimes when you look back, you realize you were doing worse than you admitted at the time.\n\nDoctor: Was there something specific, like your husband's injury, that you think might have triggered you starting to feel this way over a year ago?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. For a little while after it happened, I was just... functioning, I guess. Handling things, trying to be practical, telling myself we just had to get through the next thing and the next thing.\n\nBut then a few months after that, it was like everything caught up with me. That’s when the depression really settled in, and it just didn’t leave. So it’s been about a year and two months now, since a few months after his injury, that I’ve been feeling this way. It’s hard to explain because at first I thought it was just stress, but it became something that stayed with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt completely back to normal between episodes of depression?\n\nPatient: No, not really. That’s part of what worries me, I guess. It hasn’t felt like there were separate episodes with a clean break in between, where I was back to my normal self for a while and then it started again.\n\nSince a few months after my husband’s injury, it’s just been kind of… there. About a year and two months now. Some days are maybe a little lighter, or I can get through things better, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt completely normal in between. It’s more like it settled over me and stayed, instead of coming and going in clear episodes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period of depression in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to count it, honestly. If you mean have I had other times in my life where I was sad or down for a while, then yes, I guess there have been times like that. But if you mean a real period of depression like this one, where it felt like it took over my whole life and didn’t really let up, then I’m not sure I can say that.\n\nI keep trying to look back and separate things into neat chunks, like “that was one depression” and “this is another,” but my memory of those times isn’t that clear in that way. I know I’ve had rough patches before, and I know I didn’t always handle things well, but this current one feels different enough that I don’t want to say I’ve had multiple periods like this if I’m not sure.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I’m not sure. Maybe I’ve had low periods before, but I don’t know that I’ve had more than one true period of depression in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current feelings of depression are a return of the same depression you felt after your husband's injury, or do you feel like it's a new episode?\n\nPatient: I think it’s the same depression, honestly. It doesn’t feel like something that went away and then came back new. It’s more like it started a few months after my husband’s injury and it’s just… stayed with me since then.\n\nI keep trying to think of it in terms of episodes, because I know that’s what you’re asking, but I don’t really feel like there was a clear ending point. It’s been about a year and two months now, and even when it shifts a little from day to day, it still feels like the same thing I’ve been carrying since then.\n\nDoctor: Are there any current stressors or difficult situations in your life that you feel are contributing to how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that, because I don’t know if I can point to one current thing and say, “That’s what’s causing it.” I mean, life is stressful in the normal ways, I guess, and there are always things to worry about, but I don’t know that there’s some new situation happening right now that explains why I feel the way I do.\n\nIt’s more like… the feeling is already there, and then ordinary things feel heavier than they should. So I can’t tell if the stress is contributing to it, or if I’m just less able to handle stress because of how I’ve been feeling. I don’t want to make something up just to have an answer.\n\nSo, no, not anything clear that I’ve noticed. Nothing specific comes to mind that feels like, “This is the current stressor making everything worse.”\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of depression before the one that started after your husband's injury?\n\nPatient: Before that? I’m not really sure. I’ve had times in my life where I was down, or stressed, or just not myself for a while, but I don’t know if I would call them depression in the same way this has been.\n\nI keep thinking maybe I’m supposed to be able to look back and say, “Yes, that was an episode,” but it doesn’t feel that clear to me. It hasn’t felt like there were separate episodes with a clean break in between, where I was back to my normal self for a while and then it started again.\n\nSince a few months after my husband’s injury, it’s just been kind of… there. About a year and two months now. Some days are maybe a little lighter, or I can get through things better, but I wouldn’t say I’ve felt completely normal in between. It’s more like it settled over me and stayed, instead of coming and going in clear episodes.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period of depression in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to count it, honestly. If you mean have I had other times in my life where I was sad or down for a while, then yes, I guess there have been times like that. But if you mean a real period of depression like this one, where it felt like it took over my whole life and didn’t really let up, then I’m not sure I can say that.\n\nI keep trying to look back and separate things into neat chunks, like “that was one depression” and “this is another,” but my memory of those times isn’t that clear in that way. I know I’ve had rough patches before, and I know I didn’t always handle things well, but this current one feels different enough that I don’t want to say I’ve had multiple periods like this if I’m not sure.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I’m not sure. Maybe I’ve had low periods before, but I don’t know that I’ve had more than one true period of depression in the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your current feelings of depression are a return of the same depression you felt after your husband's injury, or do you feel like it's a new episode?\n\nPatient: I think it’s the same depression, honestly. It doesn’t feel like something that went away and then came back new. It’s more like it started a few months after my husband’s injury and it’s just… stayed with me since then.\n\nI keep trying to think of it in terms of episodes, because I know that’s what you’re asking, but I don’t really feel like there was a clear ending point. It’s been about a year and two months now, and even when it shifts a little from day to day, it still feels like the same thing I’ve been carrying since then.\n\nDoctor: Are there any current stressors or difficult situations in your life that you feel are contributing to how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that, because I don’t know if I can point to one current thing and say, “That’s what’s causing it.” I mean, life is stressful in the normal ways, I guess, and there are always things to worry about, but I don’t know that there’s some new situation happening right now that explains why I feel the way I do.\n\nIt’s more like… the feeling is already there, and then ordinary things feel heavier than they should. So I can’t tell if the stress is contributing to it, or if I’m just less able to handle stress because of how I’ve been feeling. I don’t want to make something up just to have an answer.\n\nSo, no, not anything clear that I’ve noticed. Nothing specific comes to mind that feels like, “This is the current stressor making everything worse.”\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of depression before the one that started after your husband's injury?\n\nPatient: Before that? I’m not really sure. I’ve had times in my life where I was down, or stressed, or just not myself for a while, but I don’t know if I would call them depression in the same way this has been.\n\nI keep thinking maybe I’m supposed to be able to look back and say, “Yes, that was an episode,” but it doesn’t feel that clear to me. Some of it just feels like normal hard periods in life, or times where I was overwhelmed, and I got through them somehow. This one feels more like it has a clear starting point after what happened with my husband, and then it just kept going.\n\nSo I guess my honest answer is… I don’t know. I don’t have a definite period before that where I can say, yes, that was depression. There may have been low times, but nothing I can clearly identify the way I can with this one.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself all the time, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially have gotten loose, and I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything.\n\nIt’s more that most days I just don’t really eat right. I’ll forget to eat until really late, and then when I do finally try, I’ll take a few bites and feel kind of sick, like my stomach just doesn’t want it. So I know I must be losing some weight, or at least something has changed, because my pants don’t fit the way they used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about how you would carry out the act of dying as a punishment for what you've done?\n\nPatient: I… I’ve thought about dying, yes. That’s part of why I came in, because it’s been scaring me how often it comes up in my head. A lot of mornings, or even at night before I sleep, I’ll think it would just be better if I didn’t wake up. Like if I could just not have to keep being here and keep being this person who has caused so much hurt.\n\nAnd sometimes it gets tied up with this feeling that dying would be… I don’t know, like the fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family and to my crew. Like I don’t deserve to just keep going like nothing happened. I keep replaying it and thinking maybe if I was gone, that would balance it somehow, or at least people wouldn’t have to deal with me anymore.\n\nI don’t know if that answers exactly what you’re asking. It’s not like I have some clear speech prepared about it. It’s more that the thought comes in really hard, like, “You should be gone,” or “It would be better if you didn’t wake up.” And when I’m already feeling guilty, it starts to feel almost… deserved.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any periods of depression before your husband's injury?\n\nPatient: I know I kind of answered that already, but… I really don’t know how to separate it out looking back.\n\nI’ve had hard stretches before, sure. Times where I was worn down, or unhappy, or just felt like I was dragging myself through things. But I can’t point to one and say, “That was definitely depression,” not the way I can point to what happened after my husband got hurt. That felt like there was a before and after, if that makes sense.\n\nBefore then, I think I would’ve just called it stress, or being tired, or life being heavy for a while. Maybe I wasn’t paying enough attention to myself, I don’t know. But I don’t have a clear episode in my mind that I can say started here and ended there. So my honest answer is I’m not sure. There were low times, but nothing I can confidently name as depression before his injury.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period, no matter how brief, since your husband's injury where you felt significantly better and more like your normal self?\n\nPatient: Yes… there was a period like that. Not like I was “fine,” exactly, but compared to how bad it had been, it was better enough that I noticed it.\n\nFor maybe a few weeks, I was getting up more regularly instead of staying in bed half the day. I was eating a little better too — not normal meals all the time, but at least more than just picking at something or forgetting completely. And I wasn’t crying all day every day during that stretch. I could get through more of the day without falling apart, and I remember thinking maybe I was starting to come back to myself a little.\n\nBut it wasn’t like everything cleared up. Even during that better stretch, I was still hearing the voice at work, and I still really believed clients and suppliers were tracking me. That part didn’t go away just because I was functioning a little better. I could show up and do more things, but underneath it I was still scared and still convinced something was happening around me.\n\nSo… yes, there was a better period after his injury, but it wasn’t back to normal. It was more like I could move around and eat and not cry constantly, while that other stuff was still there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. If you mean, like, separate clear episodes where I can look back and say, “That was one depression, then I got better, then another one happened,” I don’t know that I can really identify that.

I’ve had more than one low period in my life, definitely. I don’t want to make it sound like everything was perfect before and then suddenly everything changed. There were stretches where I felt worn down or sad or like I was just getting through the day because I had to. But I didn’t really keep track of it that way, and I don’t know if those counted as distinct depressions or just… life being hard, or me being overwhelmed.

The only thing I can say is that the depression after my husband’s injury felt different and much more obvious to me. That one stands out. Before that, I can’t honestly separate things into neat periods. So I guess my answer is: I’m not sure. Maybe there were low times, but I don’t know that I’ve had more than one distinct period that I could confidently call depression.

I keep going over it and over it, like I’ve caused damage and I shouldn’t get to just keep walking around while everyone else has to deal with me.
